It’s not like a vague noise or someone mumbling across the shop — it’s clear, like a man standing right beside me talking into my ear.\n\nAnd he’ll say things like I shouldn’t trust my husband, or that I shouldn’t touch certain cars. It makes it really hard to do my job because I’ll be there with my hands on a tool, and then I hear him tell me not to touch that car, and I freeze. I keep looking around to see if someone’s messing with me, but there’s nobody there, and it’s… it’s scary and embarrassing. I didn’t really want to come in, but it’s starting to feel like I can’t tell what I’m supposed to do when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something, or is it mostly criticizing or commenting on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: It’s more than just criticizing, I think. He does comment sometimes, like he’s watching what I’m doing, but the part that scares me is when he tells me not to do things. Like I’ll be at work in the garage and I’ll hear this man’s voice coming from the vents, or sometimes when I’m leaned over an engine it sounds like it’s coming from under the hood, and it’s clear — not like shop noise or someone in the next bay.\n\nHe’ll say I shouldn’t trust my husband, or he’ll tell me not to touch certain cars. And when he says that, it doesn’t feel like a random thought in my head. It sounds like somebody right next to me saying it, and I end up stopping because I don’t know if I’m supposed to listen or not. That’s really what brought me in, because it’s starting to interfere with my work and it’s frightening.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to hurt yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: Not… not like that, no. I mean, I don’t think he’s told me to hurt myself or hurt somebody else. That’s part of why I keep trying to tell myself maybe it’s just stress or the garage noise playing tricks on me, but it’s so clear that it doesn’t feel like that.\n\nWhat he does say is more like warnings or commands about what not to do — like not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. And it’s scary because it sounds like a man right there next to me, or coming through the garage vents, or sometimes from under the hood when I’m working. So even if he’s not saying “hurt someone,” it still makes me freeze up because it feels like I’m supposed to obey it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad, empty, or hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I wouldn’t say I’ve been walking around thinking, like, “everything is hopeless,” exactly. It’s more that I feel worn down and kind of overwhelmed, and sometimes embarrassed, I guess, because I don’t feel like myself.\n\nSad, maybe at moments, but I’m not sure if sad is the main word. Empty… I don’t know. I haven’t really stopped to label it that way. I’ve mostly just been trying to get through the day and keep things together, so it’s hard for me to tell what feeling is what.\n\nDoctor: Has something very stressful or traumatic happened to you recently or in the past that might be connected to when you started hearing the voice?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I’ve been trying to think about that because I know people ask that for a reason, like whether something happened and then this started, but I don’t have a clear answer.\n\nI mean, my life has stress, sure — work can be stressful, and home stuff can be stressful — but I can’t point to one big traumatic thing and say, “That’s when it began.” It doesn’t feel that neat to me. I keep going over it in my head, like maybe I missed something or maybe there’s some obvious connection I’m not seeing, but nothing really stands out.\n\nSo… I don’t know. Not recently, at least not that I can identify. And in the past, I don’t want to say nothing ever happened, because everybody has things, but I can’t connect it directly to the voice starting.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t think of my thoughts as disjointed, exactly, but sometimes when I’m trying to explain something, I can tell I’m taking too long to get to the point. Like I’ll start answering one thing and then I realize there’s this whole background to it, and if I don’t explain that part, then what I’m saying won’t make sense, so I circle around.\n\nI don’t know if other people think I’m not making sense. Nobody has said it to me that way, not directly. But I do get worried I’m not explaining it right, especially when I’m trying to talk about something that already feels confusing to me. Then I kind of over-explain, and halfway through I can hear myself doing it, but stopping feels hard because I’m afraid I’ll leave out the important part.\n\nSo… hard to express my thoughts, maybe sometimes. But I don’t know if my thoughts themselves are broken up, or if I’m just anxious about saying them clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does. It’s not always in a simple way, like the voice happens and then I immediately feel one exact thing. But I’ve noticed sometimes when it’s around, or when I’m already kind of keyed up, my mood can shoot way up or flip into irritation really fast.\n\nLike, I’ll be at work and suddenly feel like I’m on top of everything, like I could fix every car in the whole shop by myself, like nobody can keep up with me and I know exactly what needs doing. And then if somebody touches my socket set or moves something from where I had it, I don’t just get mildly annoyed — I blow up. It feels personal, like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back.\n\nAnd at home it can be the same kind of thing. I’ll feel like I’m going a hundred miles an hour, like I’m handling everything, and then my husband asks something normal, like why the dishes aren’t done, and I snap at him like he’s attacking me. In the moment it feels justified, like he doesn’t understand what I’m dealing with, but afterward I can see it was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nSo… yes, my mood changes. It can get really high, and then really irritable, especially around when the voice is happening or when I’m already wound up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a different person when the voice is talking to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’m a completely different person, but it feels like something shifts in me when it’s happening. Like I’m still me, I know I’m me, but when that man’s voice starts coming through — usually from the garage vents, or sometimes when I’m leaned over a hood and it feels like it’s coming from under there — it’s so clear that it kind of takes over the room, if that makes sense.\n\nIt doesn’t feel like a thought I’m having. It sounds like somebody is right next to me, talking straight at me, and then I start reacting like I would if an actual person were there saying those things. Especially when he tells me not to trust my husband, or tells me not to touch certain cars. I start feeling on edge, like I have to listen or at least pay attention, because ignoring it feels… risky, I guess.\n\nSo, different person? Maybe not exactly. But I do feel less like myself. More guarded. More suspicious. Like my normal judgment gets pushed to the side for a while because the voice is so convincing in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're doing things because the voice told you to, without being able to choose not to do them?\n\nPatient: It’s hard to explain, because I don’t feel like my body is being remote-controlled or anything like that. I still feel like I’m the one standing there, making the choice. But when the voice is really clear — like from the garage vents, or when I’m bent over a hood and it sounds like a man is right there next to me — it can feel like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nEspecially when he tells me not to touch certain cars. I’ll stop what I’m doing, or hesitate, or kind of back off for a minute because it feels wrong to ignore it. Same with when he says not to trust my husband — I don’t want to just believe it, but it gets in my head and I start acting more guarded.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say I can’t choose not to. But in the moment, it feels really hard to go against it, because it sounds so real. Like someone standing there warning me, not just a thought I can brush off.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I noticed it right away, because at first I’d tell myself I was just tired or busy or needed a quiet day, but then the quiet days kind of turn into stretches where I’m not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I used to be pretty involved with church stuff — the potlucks especially. I’d help plan them, bring food, stay after to clean up, talk to people. It wasn’t some huge glamorous thing, but it mattered to me. Lately I’ll see the calls or texts come in and I just… don’t answer. I tell myself I’ll call back later, and then I don’t. And then I feel embarrassed, so I avoid it more.\n\nEven at work, sometimes I’ll be in the bay and just kind of stare at my tools. Tools I know, tools I’ve used a thousand times, and I’ll just sit there looking at them like I can’t make myself care enough to pick them up. It’s not that I don’t know what to do. It’s more like the part of me that used to feel interested or motivated just goes flat.\n\nAt home too, I’ll sit in the house and not call anyone, not go anywhere, not want to do the things I used to say I liked. It’s like everything feels too far away from me, even stuff that used to feel normal. So yes… I think I have lost interest, at least in these stretches.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice is actually trying to protect you or warn you about something real?\n\nPatient: I go back and forth on that. When I’m sitting here talking to you, part of me knows it sounds… I don’t know, it sounds strange to say out loud. But when it’s happening, it really does feel like he’s warning me about something real.\n\nWhat brought me in is that I keep hearing this man’s voice at work, and it’s not like an inside thought or my own worries. It comes from the garage vents sometimes, like above or off to the side, and other times when I’m leaned over under the hood it sounds like it’s coming from right there, under the hood, clear as somebody standing next to me. And he’ll say things like not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. So in the moment, yeah, it feels like maybe he knows something I don’t.\n\nI don’t want to sound like I’m just blindly believing it, because I do question it afterward. But when it’s that clear, and it’s saying “don’t touch that” or warning me about my husband, it feels hard to treat it like nothing. It feels like if I ignore it and something happens, then that would be on me. So… yes, sometimes I do believe it’s trying to protect me, or at least warn me. Even if another part of me is scared that I don’t really know where it’s coming from.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever give you instructions on what actions to take?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes it does. Not like, “go do this exact complicated thing,” but it gives me instructions in the sense that it tells me what not to do.\n\nThe main thing is at work. I’ll hear this man’s voice coming from the garage vents, or sometimes when I’m under the hood it sounds like it’s right there with me, clear as somebody standing beside me. And he’ll say not to touch certain cars. Like I’ll be about to start working on one, or reach for something, and I hear it, and then I stop. I might step back or just freeze for a second because it feels like I’m being told there’s a reason I shouldn’t.\n\nAnd he’s told me not to trust my husband too. That’s not an action like picking up a tool, but it still affects what I do, because then I get guarded or start questioning things.\n\nSo yes, I guess the answer is yes — he does give instructions, mostly warnings or “don’t do that” kinds of things. It sounds so real when it happens that it’s hard not to react to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been showing less emotion than usual, even when something good or bad happens?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to explain because it’s not like I’m choosing to be cold or I don’t understand that something matters. It’s more like my face and my voice don’t always catch up to what’s happening, or maybe I’m not feeling it the way I normally would.\n\nThere are these stretches where I just get really flat. Like people will call or text me, and I see it, and I know I should answer, especially people from church or friends who are probably just checking in. And I just… don’t. I let it sit there. Then I feel bad, but even the feeling bad is kind of dull, like it’s under a blanket.\n\nThe church potlucks are a good example. I used to help with those a lot — planning, bringing food, talking to people, staying after. It used to make me feel connected, like I was part of something. Now I’ll skip them, even when I know I should go, and I don’t have some big reaction about missing it. I just sit at home. Sometimes I’m sitting there knowing I’m missing something I used to care about, and I still can’t make myself feel much.\n\nAt work it happens too. I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and these are tools I know, work I know how to do, and I’ll just look at them like they’re somebody else’s. Like I can’t make myself care enough to start. I don’t get excited, I don’t get frustrated in the normal way… I just kind of go blank.\n\nSo yes, less emotion, I guess. Or less showing it. Maybe both. Everything gets muted.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you show less emotion than usual when the voice is not present?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think it still happens even when I’m not hearing him. It’s not only when the voice is there and I’m scared or distracted. There are whole stretches where it’s just me, no voice right then, and I still feel… flat, I guess. Like I’m moving through the day but not really attached to anything.\n\nLike with my phone — I’ll see people have called or texted, and it’s not because the voice is telling me not to answer in that moment. I just look at it and can’t make myself care enough to pick it up, even though I know I should. Or the church potlucks, I used to help run those, like actually be involved and talk to people and bring things, and now I’ll just skip them and sit in the house. Not crying, not angry, just sitting there like everything is far away.\n\nAnd at work too, even when it’s quiet and nothing is happening, I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and I know what I’m supposed to do, I know those tools, but I feel like I can’t make myself care about any of it. My face probably looks blank. My voice gets kind of plain. So… yeah, it’s not only tied to the voice. It’s there on its own sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling more emotional or connected to things when you're not in one of those flat stretches?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. I mean… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m flat every single second, because I’m not. There are times where I do feel more like myself, or I’ll notice something matters to me again for a little while.\n\nBut when those stretches hit, it’s like everything just shuts down. I won’t call anyone back, even if I know they care about me. I’ll see the phone light up and just let it go. And the church potlucks… I used to help with those, not just show up, but actually help run things, talk to people, bring food, clean up after. When I’m not in that flat place, I can remember why that mattered. I can even miss it a little.\n\nBut during it, I just skip it. I sit in the house and don’t really do anything with the feeling. Or at work, I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and I know exactly what I’m supposed to be doing, but it’s like I can’t make myself care enough to start. It feels disconnected, like there’s a wall between me and everything I normally care about.\n\nSo yes, I guess there are times I feel more connected than that. But the flat stretches are strong enough that they kind of take over when they’re happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic, irritable, or abnormally happy for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s not just like a normal good mood, either. It’s more like I get this rush where I feel like I’m on top of everything, like I could handle anything anybody throws at me.\n\nAt work, I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town if they just bring them to my bay. I’ll feel fast and sharp and almost… untouchable, I guess. Like nobody else is keeping up with me. And in those times, if something small happens, it can flip really quick. Like if another tech moves my socket set or puts something back wrong, I don’t just get annoyed — I blow up like they’re doing it on purpose, like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back.\n\nAnd at home it’s the same kind of thing. I can be feeling really up, really energized, like I’ve got all these things I’m going to do, and then if my husband asks something simple like why the dishes aren’t done, I take it like he’s criticizing me or trying to box me in. I get mad way bigger than the situation calls for.\n\nSo yes… there have been periods like that. It feels good at first, honestly, like I’m powerful and everything is possible, but then I get really irritable if anyone gets in my way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic or abnormally happy that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. And I know I kind of said this already, but it’s hard to explain without making it sound like I’m just “in a good mood,” because it’s not really that. It’s more like I’m flying high, like I’m suddenly faster than everybody else and I can handle anything.\n\nLike at work, I’ll feel like I can fix every car in town if they’d just bring them to my bay. I’ll be moving around with all this energy, thinking I know exactly what needs doing, like nobody should question me because I’m already three steps ahead. And then if somebody does something small — like another tech moves my socket set or puts it back wrong — I don’t just think, “Oh, that’s annoying.” I blow up. I feel like they’re trying to hold me down or slow me up on purpose.\n\nAnd at home it can be the same thing. I’ll be feeling all charged up, like I’m on some kind of roll, and then my husband asks something simple like why the dishes aren’t done, and I take it like he’s trying to criticize me or box me in. I get way more angry than I should. So yeah, there have been times like that where I’m really up, really energized, and then it flips into this irritated feeling if anybody gets in my way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
They come and go in the sense that I can feel it rise and fall, like some days it’s louder than others… but it hasn’t felt like something that just happened once and then was over.

The long stretches — the ones where I’m barely sleeping, and my mind is full of plans, and I’m just going nonstop like I have to keep moving — that’s been going on for… around eleven months now, I think. Almost a year, which sounds worse when I say it out loud.

It’s not every single minute at the exact same level. Like, there are days where I can seem more normal from the outside, or I might crash a little and think maybe it’s finally settling down. But then it picks right back up again — the energy, the irritability, the feeling like I need to be doing something or planning something or pushing ahead. So I guess I’d say it comes in waves, but the whole thing has been stretched out over about eleven months.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F25.1, F31.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… mostly because I’m hearing this man’s voice at work, and it’s gotten to where I can’t just ignore it anymore. I work around cars, in the garage, and it sounds like it’s coming through the vents sometimes, like from the garage vents above me, and other times it’s like it’s right under the hood when I’m leaning in to look at an engine. It’s not like a vague noise or someone mumbling across the shop — it’s clear, like a man standing right beside me talking into my ear.\n\nAnd he’ll say things like I shouldn’t trust my husband, or that I shouldn’t touch certain cars. It makes it really hard to do my job because I’ll be there with my hands on a tool, and then I hear him tell me not to touch that car, and I freeze. I keep looking around to see if someone’s messing with me, but there’s nobody there, and it’s… it’s scary and embarrassing. I didn’t really want to come in, but it’s starting to feel like I can’t tell what I’m supposed to do when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something, or is it mostly criticizing or commenting on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: It’s more than just criticizing, I think. He does comment sometimes, like he’s watching what I’m doing, but the part that scares me is when he tells me not to do things. Like I’ll be at work in the garage and I’ll hear this man’s voice coming from the vents, or sometimes when I’m leaned over an engine it sounds like it’s coming from under the hood, and it’s clear — not like shop noise or someone in the next bay.\n\nHe’ll say I shouldn’t trust my husband, or he’ll tell me not to touch certain cars. And when he says that, it doesn’t feel like a random thought in my head. It sounds like somebody right next to me saying it, and I end up stopping because I don’t know if I’m supposed to listen or not. That’s really what brought me in, because it’s starting to interfere with my work and it’s frightening.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to hurt yourself or someone else?\n\nPatient: Not… not like that, no. I mean, I don’t think he’s told me to hurt myself or hurt somebody else. That’s part of why I keep trying to tell myself maybe it’s just stress or the garage noise playing tricks on me, but it’s so clear that it doesn’t feel like that.\n\nWhat he does say is more like warnings or commands about what not to do — like not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. And it’s scary because it sounds like a man right there next to me, or coming through the garage vents, or sometimes from under the hood when I’m working. So even if he’s not saying “hurt someone,” it still makes me freeze up because it feels like I’m supposed to obey it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad, empty, or hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I wouldn’t say I’ve been walking around thinking, like, “everything is hopeless,” exactly. It’s more that I feel worn down and kind of overwhelmed, and sometimes embarrassed, I guess, because I don’t feel like myself.\n\nSad, maybe at moments, but I’m not sure if sad is the main word. Empty… I don’t know. I haven’t really stopped to label it that way. I’ve mostly just been trying to get through the day and keep things together, so it’s hard for me to tell what feeling is what.\n\nDoctor: Has something very stressful or traumatic happened to you recently or in the past that might be connected to when you started hearing the voice?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I’ve been trying to think about that because I know people ask that for a reason, like whether something happened and then this started, but I don’t have a clear answer.\n\nI mean, my life has stress, sure — work can be stressful, and home stuff can be stressful — but I can’t point to one big traumatic thing and say, “That’s when it began.” It doesn’t feel that neat to me. I keep going over it in my head, like maybe I missed something or maybe there’s some obvious connection I’m not seeing, but nothing really stands out.\n\nSo… I don’t know. Not recently, at least not that I can identify. And in the past, I don’t want to say nothing ever happened, because everybody has things, but I can’t connect it directly to the voice starting.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t think of my thoughts as disjointed, exactly, but sometimes when I’m trying to explain something, I can tell I’m taking too long to get to the point. Like I’ll start answering one thing and then I realize there’s this whole background to it, and if I don’t explain that part, then what I’m saying won’t make sense, so I circle around.\n\nI don’t know if other people think I’m not making sense. Nobody has said it to me that way, not directly. But I do get worried I’m not explaining it right, especially when I’m trying to talk about something that already feels confusing to me. Then I kind of over-explain, and halfway through I can hear myself doing it, but stopping feels hard because I’m afraid I’ll leave out the important part.\n\nSo… hard to express my thoughts, maybe sometimes. But I don’t know if my thoughts themselves are broken up, or if I’m just anxious about saying them clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does. It’s not always in a simple way, like the voice happens and then I immediately feel one exact thing. But I’ve noticed sometimes when it’s around, or when I’m already kind of keyed up, my mood can shoot way up or flip into irritation really fast.\n\nLike, I’ll be at work and suddenly feel like I’m on top of everything, like I could fix every car in the whole shop by myself, like nobody can keep up with me and I know exactly what needs doing. And then if somebody touches my socket set or moves something from where I had it, I don’t just get mildly annoyed — I blow up. It feels personal, like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back.\n\nAnd at home it can be the same kind of thing. I’ll feel like I’m going a hundred miles an hour, like I’m handling everything, and then my husband asks something normal, like why the dishes aren’t done, and I snap at him like he’s attacking me. In the moment it feels justified, like he doesn’t understand what I’m dealing with, but afterward I can see it was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nSo… yes, my mood changes. It can get really high, and then really irritable, especially around when the voice is happening or when I’m already wound up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a different person when the voice is talking to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’m a completely different person, but it feels like something shifts in me when it’s happening. Like I’m still me, I know I’m me, but when that man’s voice starts coming through — usually from the garage vents, or sometimes when I’m leaned over a hood and it feels like it’s coming from under there — it’s so clear that it kind of takes over the room, if that makes sense.\n\nIt doesn’t feel like a thought I’m having. It sounds like somebody is right next to me, talking straight at me, and then I start reacting like I would if an actual person were there saying those things. Especially when he tells me not to trust my husband, or tells me not to touch certain cars. I start feeling on edge, like I have to listen or at least pay attention, because ignoring it feels… risky, I guess.\n\nSo, different person? Maybe not exactly. But I do feel less like myself. More guarded. More suspicious. Like my normal judgment gets pushed to the side for a while because the voice is so convincing in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're doing things because the voice told you to, without being able to choose not to do them?\n\nPatient: It’s hard to explain, because I don’t feel like my body is being remote-controlled or anything like that. I still feel like I’m the one standing there, making the choice. But when the voice is really clear — like from the garage vents, or when I’m bent over a hood and it sounds like a man is right there next to me — it can feel like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nEspecially when he tells me not to touch certain cars. I’ll stop what I’m doing, or hesitate, or kind of back off for a minute because it feels wrong to ignore it. Same with when he says not to trust my husband — I don’t want to just believe it, but it gets in my head and I start acting more guarded.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say I can’t choose not to. But in the moment, it feels really hard to go against it, because it sounds so real. Like someone standing there warning me, not just a thought I can brush off.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I noticed it right away, because at first I’d tell myself I was just tired or busy or needed a quiet day, but then the quiet days kind of turn into stretches where I’m not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I used to be pretty involved with church stuff — the potlucks especially. I’d help plan them, bring food, stay after to clean up, talk to people. It wasn’t some huge glamorous thing, but it mattered to me. Lately I’ll see the calls or texts come in and I just… don’t answer. I tell myself I’ll call back later, and then I don’t. And then I feel embarrassed, so I avoid it more.\n\nEven at work, sometimes I’ll be in the bay and just kind of stare at my tools. Tools I know, tools I’ve used a thousand times, and I’ll just sit there looking at them like I can’t make myself care enough to pick them up. It’s not that I don’t know what to do. It’s more like the part of me that used to feel interested or motivated just goes flat.\n\nAt home too, I’ll sit in the house and not call anyone, not go anywhere, not want to do the things I used to say I liked. It’s like everything feels too far away from me, even stuff that used to feel normal. So yes… I think I have lost interest, at least in these stretches.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice is actually trying to protect you or warn you about something real?\n\nPatient: I go back and forth on that. When I’m sitting here talking to you, part of me knows it sounds… I don’t know, it sounds strange to say out loud. But when it’s happening, it really does feel like he’s warning me about something real.\n\nWhat brought me in is that I keep hearing this man’s voice at work, and it’s not like an inside thought or my own worries. It comes from the garage vents sometimes, like above or off to the side, and other times when I’m leaned over under the hood it sounds like it’s coming from right there, under the hood, clear as somebody standing next to me. And he’ll say things like not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. So in the moment, yeah, it feels like maybe he knows something I don’t.\n\nI don’t want to sound like I’m just blindly believing it, because I do question it afterward. But when it’s that clear, and it’s saying “don’t touch that” or warning me about my husband, it feels hard to treat it like nothing. It feels like if I ignore it and something happens, then that would be on me. So… yes, sometimes I do believe it’s trying to protect me, or at least warn me. Even if another part of me is scared that I don’t really know where it’s coming from.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever give you instructions on what actions to take?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes it does. Not like, “go do this exact complicated thing,” but it gives me instructions in the sense that it tells me what not to do.\n\nThe main thing is at work. I’ll hear this man’s voice coming from the garage vents, or sometimes when I’m under the hood it sounds like it’s right there with me, clear as somebody standing beside me. And he’ll say not to touch certain cars. Like I’ll be about to start working on one, or reach for something, and I hear it, and then I stop. I might step back or just freeze for a second because it feels like I’m being told there’s a reason I shouldn’t.\n\nAnd he’s told me not to trust my husband too. That’s not an action like picking up a tool, but it still affects what I do, because then I get guarded or start questioning things.\n\nSo yes, I guess the answer is yes — he does give instructions, mostly warnings or “don’t do that” kinds of things. It sounds so real when it happens that it’s hard not to react to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been showing less emotion than usual, even when something good or bad happens?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to explain because it’s not like I’m choosing to be cold or I don’t understand that something matters. It’s more like my face and my voice don’t always catch up to what’s happening, or maybe I’m not feeling it the way I normally would.\n\nThere are these stretches where I just get really flat. Like people will call or text me, and I see it, and I know I should answer, especially people from church or friends who are probably just checking in. And I just… don’t. I let it sit there. Then I feel bad, but even the feeling bad is kind of dull, like it’s under a blanket.\n\nThe church potlucks are a good example. I used to help with those a lot — planning, bringing food, talking to people, staying after. It used to make me feel connected, like I was part of something. Now I’ll skip them, even when I know I should go, and I don’t have some big reaction about missing it. I just sit at home. Sometimes I’m sitting there knowing I’m missing something I used to care about, and I still can’t make myself feel much.\n\nAt work it happens too. I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and these are tools I know, work I know how to do, and I’ll just look at them like they’re somebody else’s. Like I can’t make myself care enough to start. I don’t get excited, I don’t get frustrated in the normal way… I just kind of go blank.\n\nSo yes, less emotion, I guess. Or less showing it. Maybe both. Everything gets muted.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you show less emotion than usual when the voice is not present?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think it still happens even when I’m not hearing him. It’s not only when the voice is there and I’m scared or distracted. There are whole stretches where it’s just me, no voice right then, and I still feel… flat, I guess. Like I’m moving through the day but not really attached to anything.\n\nLike with my phone — I’ll see people have called or texted, and it’s not because the voice is telling me not to answer in that moment. I just look at it and can’t make myself care enough to pick it up, even though I know I should. Or the church potlucks, I used to help run those, like actually be involved and talk to people and bring things, and now I’ll just skip them and sit in the house. Not crying, not angry, just sitting there like everything is far away.\n\nAnd at work too, even when it’s quiet and nothing is happening, I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and I know what I’m supposed to do, I know those tools, but I feel like I can’t make myself care about any of it. My face probably looks blank. My voice gets kind of plain. So… yeah, it’s not only tied to the voice. It’s there on its own sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling more emotional or connected to things when you're not in one of those flat stretches?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. I mean… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m flat every single second, because I’m not. There are times where I do feel more like myself, or I’ll notice something matters to me again for a little while.\n\nBut when those stretches hit, it’s like everything just shuts down. I won’t call anyone back, even if I know they care about me. I’ll see the phone light up and just let it go. And the church potlucks… I used to help with those, not just show up, but actually help run things, talk to people, bring food, clean up after. When I’m not in that flat place, I can remember why that mattered. I can even miss it a little.\n\nBut during it, I just skip it. I sit in the house and don’t really do anything with the feeling. Or at work, I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and I know exactly what I’m supposed to be doing, but it’s like I can’t make myself care enough to start. It feels disconnected, like there’s a wall between me and everything I normally care about.\n\nSo yes, I guess there are times I feel more connected than that. But the flat stretches are strong enough that they kind of take over when they’re happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic, irritable, or abnormally happy for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s not just like a normal good mood, either. It’s more like I get this rush where I feel like I’m on top of everything, like I could handle anything anybody throws at me.\n\nAt work, I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town if they just bring them to my bay. I’ll feel fast and sharp and almost… untouchable, I guess. Like nobody else is keeping up with me. And in those times, if something small happens, it can flip really quick. Like if another tech moves my socket set or puts something back wrong, I don’t just get annoyed — I blow up like they’re doing it on purpose, like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back.\n\nAnd at home it’s the same kind of thing. I can be feeling really up, really energized, like I’ve got all these things I’m going to do, and then if my husband asks something simple like why the dishes aren’t done, I take it like he’s criticizing me or trying to box me in. I get mad way bigger than the situation calls for.\n\nSo yes… there have been periods like that. It feels good at first, honestly, like I’m powerful and everything is possible, but then I get really irritable if anyone gets in my way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic or abnormally happy that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. And I know I kind of said this already, but it’s hard to explain without making it sound like I’m just “in a good mood,” because it’s not really that. It’s more like I’m flying high, like I’m suddenly faster than everybody else and I can handle anything.\n\nLike at work, I’ll feel like I can fix every car in town if they’d just bring them to my bay. I’ll be moving around with all this energy, thinking I know exactly what needs doing, like nobody should question me because I’m already three steps ahead. And then if somebody does something small — like another tech moves my socket set or puts it back wrong — I don’t just think, “Oh, that’s annoying.” I blow up. I feel like they’re trying to hold me down or slow me up on purpose.\n\nAnd at home it can be the same thing. I’ll be feeling all charged up, like I’m on some kind of roll, and then my husband asks something simple like why the dishes aren’t done, and I take it like he’s trying to criticize me or box me in. I get way more angry than I should. So yeah, there have been times like that where I’m really up, really energized, and then it flips into this irritated feeling if anybody gets in my way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of extreme energy and irritability come and go, or are they more constant?\n\nPatient: They come and go in the sense that I can feel it rise and fall, like some days it’s louder than others… but it hasn’t felt like something that just happened once and then was over.\n\nThe long stretches — the ones where I’m barely sleeping, and my mind is full of plans, and I’m just going nonstop like I have to keep moving — that’s been going on for… around eleven months now, I think. Almost a year, which sounds worse when I say it out loud.\n\nIt’s not every single minute at the exact same level. Like, there are days where I can seem more normal from the outside, or I might crash a little and think maybe it’s finally settling down. But then it picks right back up again — the energy, the irritability, the feeling like I need to be doing something or planning something or pushing ahead. So I guess I’d say it comes in waves, but the whole thing has been stretched out over about eleven months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Nobody has said it to me that way, not directly. But I do get worried I’m not explaining it right, especially when I’m trying to talk about something that already feels confusing to me. It feels personal, like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back.\n\nAnd at home it can be the same kind of thing. I’ll feel like I’m going a hundred miles an hour, like I’m handling everything, and then my husband asks something normal, like why the dishes aren’t done, and I snap at him like he’s attacking me. In the moment it feels justified, like he doesn’t understand what I’m dealing with, but afterward I can see it was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nSo… yes, my mood changes. It can get really high, and then really irritable, especially around when the voice is happening or when I’m already wound up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a different person when the voice is talking to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’m a completely different person, but it feels like something shifts in me when it’s happening. Like I’m still me, I know I’m me, but when that man’s voice starts coming through — usually from the garage vents, or sometimes when I’m leaned over a hood and it feels like it’s coming from under there — it’s so clear that it kind of takes over the room, if that makes sense.\n\nIt doesn’t feel like a thought I’m having. It sounds like somebody is right next to me, talking straight at me, and then I start reacting like I would if an actual person were there saying those things. Especially when he tells me not to trust my husband, or tells me not to touch certain cars. I start feeling on edge, like I have to listen or at least pay attention, because ignoring it feels… risky, I guess.\n\nSo, different person? Maybe not exactly. But I do feel less like myself. More guarded. More suspicious. Like my normal judgment gets pushed to the side for a while because the voice is so convincing in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're doing things because the voice told you to, without being able to choose not to do them?\n\nPatient: It’s hard to explain, because I don’t feel like my body is being remote-controlled or anything like that. I still feel like I’m the one standing there, making the choice. But when the voice is really clear — like from the garage vents, or when I’m bent over a hood and it sounds like a man is right there next to me — it can feel like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nEspecially when he tells me not to touch certain cars. I’ll stop what I’m doing, or hesitate, or kind of back off for a minute because it feels wrong to ignore it. Same with when he says not to trust my husband — I don’t want to just believe it, but it gets in my head and I start acting more guarded.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say I can’t choose not to. But in the moment, it feels really hard to go against it, because it sounds so real. Like someone standing there warning me, not just a thought I can brush off.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I noticed it right away, because at first I’d tell myself I was just tired or busy or needed a quiet day, but then the quiet days kind of turn into stretches where I’m not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I used to be pretty involved with church stuff — the potlucks especially. I’d help plan them, bring food, stay after to clean up, talk to people. It wasn’t some huge glamorous thing, but it mattered to me. Lately I’ll see the calls or texts come in and I just… don’t answer. I tell myself I’ll call back later, and then I don’t. And then I feel embarrassed, so I avoid it more.\n\nEven at work, sometimes I’ll be in the bay and just kind of stare at my tools. Tools I know, tools I’ve used a thousand times, and I’ll just sit there looking at them like I can’t make myself care enough to pick them up. It’s not that I don’t know what to do. It’s more like the part of me that used to feel interested or motivated just goes flat.\n\nAt home too, I’ll sit in the house and not call anyone, not go anywhere, not want to do the things I used to say I liked. It’s like everything feels too far away from me, even stuff that used to feel normal. So yes… I think I have lost interest, at least in these stretches.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice is actually trying to protect you or warn you about something real?\n\nPatient: I go back and forth on that. When I’m sitting here talking to you, part of me knows it sounds… I don’t know, it sounds strange to say out loud. But when it’s happening, it really does feel like he’s warning me about something real.\n\nWhat brought me in is that I keep hearing this man’s voice at work, and it’s not like an inside thought or my own worries. It comes from the garage vents sometimes, like above or off to the side, and other times when I’m leaned over under the hood it sounds like it’s coming from right there, under the hood, clear as somebody standing next to me. And he’ll say things like not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. So in the moment, yeah, it feels like maybe he knows something I don’t.\n\nI don’t want to sound like I’m just blindly believing it, because I do question it afterward. But when it’s that clear, and it’s saying “don’t touch that” or warning me about my husband, it feels hard to treat it like nothing. It feels like if I ignore it and something happens, then that would be on me. So… yes, sometimes I do believe it’s trying to protect me, or at least warn me. Even if another part of me is scared that I don’t really know where it’s coming from.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever give you instructions on what actions to take?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes it does. Not like, “go do this exact complicated thing,” but it gives me instructions in the sense that it tells me what not to do.\n\nThe main thing is at work. I’ll hear this man’s voice coming from the garage vents, or sometimes when I’m under the hood it sounds like it’s right there with me, clear as somebody standing beside me. And he’ll say not to touch certain cars. Like I’ll be about to start working on one, or reach for something, and I hear it, and then I stop. I might step back or just freeze for a second because it feels like I’m being told there’s a reason I shouldn’t.\n\nAnd he’s told me not to trust my husband too. That’s not an action like picking up a tool, but it still affects what I do, because then I get guarded or start questioning things.\n\nSo yes, I guess the answer is yes — he does give instructions, mostly warnings or “don’t do that” kinds of things. It sounds so real when it happens that it’s hard not to react to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been showing less emotion than usual, even when something good or bad happens?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to explain because it’s not like I’m choosing to be cold or I don’t understand that something matters. It’s more like my face and my voice don’t always catch up to what’s happening, or maybe I’m not feeling it the way I normally would.\n\nThere are these stretches where I just get really flat. Like people will call or text me, and I see it, and I know I should answer, especially people from church or friends who are probably just checking in. And I just… don’t. I let it sit there. Then I feel bad, but even the feeling bad is kind of dull, like it’s under a blanket.\n\nThe church potlucks are a good example. I used to help with those a lot — planning, bringing food, talking to people, staying after. It used to make me feel connected, like I was part of something. Now I’ll skip them, even when I know I should go, and I don’t have some big reaction about missing it. I just sit at home. Sometimes I’m sitting there knowing I’m missing something I used to care about, and I still can’t make myself feel much.\n\nAt work it happens too. I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and these are tools I know, work I know how to do, and I’ll just look at them like they’re somebody else’s. Like I can’t make myself care enough to start. I don’t get excited, I don’t get frustrated in the normal way… I just kind of go blank.\n\nSo yes, less emotion, I guess. Or less showing it. Maybe both. Everything gets muted.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you show less emotion than usual when the voice is not present?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think it still happens even when I’m not hearing him. It’s not only when the voice is there and I’m scared or distracted. There are whole stretches where it’s just me, no voice right then, and I still feel… flat, I guess. Like I’m moving through the day but not really attached to anything.\n\nLike with my phone — I’ll see people have called or texted, and it’s not because the voice is telling me not to answer in that moment. I just look at it and can’t make myself care enough to pick it up, even though I know I should. Or the church potlucks, I used to help run those, like actually be involved and talk to people and bring things, and now I’ll just skip them and sit in the house. Not crying, not angry, just sitting there like everything is far away.\n\nAnd at work too, even when it’s quiet and nothing is happening, I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and I know what I’m supposed to do, I know those tools, but I feel like I can’t make myself care about any of it. My face probably looks blank. My voice gets kind of plain. So… yeah, it’s not only tied to the voice. It’s there on its own sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling more emotional or connected to things when you're not in one of those flat stretches?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. I mean… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m flat every single second, because I’m not. There are times where I do feel more like myself, or I’ll notice something matters to me again for a little while.\n\nBut when those stretches hit, it’s like everything just shuts down. I won’t call anyone back, even if I know they care about me. I’ll see the phone light up and just let it go. And the church potlucks… I used to help with those, not just show up, but actually help run things, talk to people, bring food, clean up after. When I’m not in that flat place, I can remember why that mattered. I can even miss it a little.\n\nBut during it, I just skip it. I sit in the house and don’t really do anything with the feeling. Or at work, I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and I know exactly what I’m supposed to be doing, but it’s like I can’t make myself care enough to start. It feels disconnected, like there’s a wall between me and everything I normally care about.\n\nSo yes, I guess there are times I feel more connected than that. But the flat stretches are strong enough that they kind of take over when they’re happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic, irritable, or abnormally happy for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s not just like a normal good mood, either. It’s more like I get this rush where I feel like I’m on top of everything, like I could handle anything anybody throws at me.\n\nAt work, I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town if they just bring them to my bay. I’ll feel fast and sharp and almost… untouchable, I guess. Like nobody else is keeping up with me. And in those times, if something small happens, it can flip really quick. Like if another tech moves my socket set or puts something back wrong, I don’t just get annoyed — I blow up like they’re doing it on purpose, like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back.\n\nAnd at home it’s the same kind of thing. I can be feeling really up, really energized, like I’ve got all these things I’m going to do, and then if my husband asks something simple like why the dishes aren’t done, I take it like he’s criticizing me or trying to box me in. I get mad way bigger than the situation calls for.\n\nSo yes… there have been periods like that. It feels good at first, honestly, like I’m powerful and everything is possible, but then I get really irritable if anyone gets in my way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic or abnormally happy that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. And I know I kind of said this already, but it’s hard to explain without making it sound like I’m just “in a good mood,” because it’s not really that. It’s more like I’m flying high, like I’m suddenly faster than everybody else and I can handle anything.\n\nLike at work, I’ll feel like I can fix every car in town if they’d just bring them to my bay. I’ll be moving around with all this energy, thinking I know exactly what needs doing, like nobody should question me because I’m already three steps ahead. And then if somebody does something small — like another tech moves my socket set or puts it back wrong — I don’t just think, “Oh, that’s annoying.” I blow up. I feel like they’re trying to hold me down or slow me up on purpose.\n\nAnd at home it can be the same thing. I’ll be feeling all charged up, like I’m on some kind of roll, and then my husband asks something simple like why the dishes aren’t done, and I take it like he’s trying to criticize me or box me in. I get way more angry than I should. So yeah, there have been times like that where I’m really up, really energized, and then it flips into this irritated feeling if anybody gets in my way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of extreme energy and irritability come and go, or are they more constant?\n\nPatient: They come and go in the sense that I can feel it rise and fall, like some days it’s louder than others… but it hasn’t felt like something that just happened once and then was over.\n\nThe long stretches — the ones where I’m barely sleeping, and my mind is full of plans, and I’m just going nonstop like I have to keep moving — that’s been going on for… around eleven months now, I think. Almost a year, which sounds worse when I say it out loud.\n\nIt’s not every single minute at the exact same level. Like, there are days where I can seem more normal from the outside, or I might crash a little and think maybe it’s finally settling down. But then it picks right back up again — the energy, the irritability, the feeling like I need to be doing something or planning something or pushing ahead. So I guess I’d say it comes in waves, but the whole thing has been stretched out over about eleven months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic or abnormally happy that lasted for at least one week?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely at least a week. Honestly, it’s been way longer than that.\n\nThe bigger stretches where I’m barely sleeping and my head is just full of plans, like I can’t stop thinking about what I’m going to do next, and I’m moving nonstop… that’s been going on for around eleven months now. Not every single hour feels exactly the same, but it’s not like it was just a few days and then gone. It’s been this long, drawn-out thing where the energy and the racing plans keep coming back and I just keep pushing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Like if another tech moves my socket set or puts something back wrong, I don’t just get annoyed — I blow up like they’re doing it on purpose, like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back.\n\nAnd at home it’s the same kind of thing. I can be feeling really up, really energized, like I’ve got all these things I’m going to do, and then if my husband asks something simple like why the dishes aren’t done, I take it like he’s criticizing me or trying to box me in. I get mad way bigger than the situation calls for.\n\nSo yes… there have been periods like that. It feels good at first, honestly, like I’m powerful and everything is possible, but then I get really irritable if anyone gets in my way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic or abnormally happy that lasted for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. I’ll be moving around with all this energy, thinking I know exactly what needs doing, like nobody should question me because I’m already three steps ahead. And then if somebody does something small — like another tech moves my socket set or puts it back wrong — I don’t just think, “Oh, that’s annoying.” I blow up. I feel like they’re trying to hold me down or slow me up on purpose.\n\nAnd at home it can be the same thing. I’ll be feeling all charged up, like I’m on some kind of roll, and then my husband asks something simple like why the dishes aren’t done, and I take it like he’s trying to criticize me or box me in. I get way more angry than I should. So yeah, there have been times like that where I’m really up, really energized, and then it flips into this irritated feeling if anybody gets in my way.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these periods of extreme energy and irritability come and go, or are they more constant?\n\nPatient: They come and go in the sense that I can feel it rise and fall, like some days it’s louder than others… but it hasn’t felt like something that just happened once and then was over.\n\nThe long stretches — the ones where I’m barely sleeping, and my mind is full of plans, and I’m just going nonstop like I have to keep moving — that’s been going on for… around eleven months now, I think. Almost a year, which sounds worse when I say it out loud.\n\nIt’s not every single minute at the exact same level. Yes. Four days, yes — that’s not hard for me to say. It’s gone on longer than that at times.

It’s like I get this feeling where I’m flying high, like I can handle anything, like if every car in town rolled into the shop I could fix them all myself and still have energy left. And in my head it feels clear in the moment, like everyone else is moving too slow or getting in the way.

But it doesn’t stay just “happy” or good. It can turn sharp really fast. Like if one of the other techs moves my socket set or puts something back wrong, I don’t react like it’s just annoying — I blow up, like they’re doing it on purpose to slow me down or hold me down. Or at home, if my husband asks why the dishes aren’t done, I take it like he’s criticizing me or trying to box me in. I know that probably sounds like a small thing, but in that state it feels huge.

So yes, definitely at least four days in a row. It’s that high, charged-up feeling, and then the anger comes right behind it if anyone gets in my way.

I didn’t really want to come in, but it’s starting to feel like I can’t tell what I’m supposed to do when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever tell you to do something, or is it mostly criticizing or commenting on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: It’s more than just criticizing, I think. He does comment sometimes, like he’s watching what I’m doing, but the part that scares me is when he tells me not to do things. Like I’ll be at work in the garage and I’ll hear this man’s voice coming from the vents, or sometimes when I’m leaned over an engine it sounds like it’s coming from under the hood, and it’s clear — not like shop noise or someone in the next bay.\n\nHe’ll say I shouldn’t trust my husband, or he’ll tell me not to touch certain cars. I mean, I don’t think he’s told me to hurt myself or hurt somebody else. That’s part of why I keep trying to tell myself maybe it’s just stress or the garage noise playing tricks on me, but it’s so clear that it doesn’t feel like that.\n\nWhat he does say is more like warnings or commands about what not to do — like not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. And it’s scary because it sounds like a man right there next to me, or coming through the garage vents, or sometimes from under the hood when I’m working. So even if he’s not saying “hurt someone,” it still makes me freeze up because it feels like I’m supposed to obey it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad, empty, or hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I wouldn’t say I’ve been walking around thinking, like, “everything is hopeless,” exactly. It’s more that I feel worn down and kind of overwhelmed, and sometimes embarrassed, I guess, because I don’t feel like myself.\n\nSad, maybe at moments, but I’m not sure if sad is the main word. Empty… I don’t know. I haven’t really stopped to label it that way. I’ve mostly just been trying to get through the day and keep things together, so it’s hard for me to tell what feeling is what.\n\nDoctor: Has something very stressful or traumatic happened to you recently or in the past that might be connected to when you started hearing the voice?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I’ve been trying to think about that because I know people ask that for a reason, like whether something happened and then this started, but I don’t have a clear answer.\n\nI mean, my life has stress, sure — work can be stressful, and home stuff can be stressful — but I can’t point to one big traumatic thing and say, “That’s when it began.” It doesn’t feel that neat to me. I keep going over it in my head, like maybe I missed something or maybe there’s some obvious connection I’m not seeing, but nothing really stands out.\n\nSo… I don’t know. Not recently, at least not that I can identify. And in the past, I don’t want to say nothing ever happened, because everybody has things, but I can’t connect it directly to the voice starting.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it hard to express your thoughts or feel like your thoughts are disjointed or don't make sense to others?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I don’t think of my thoughts as disjointed, exactly, but sometimes when I’m trying to explain something, I can tell I’m taking too long to get to the point. Like I’ll start answering one thing and then I realize there’s this whole background to it, and if I don’t explain that part, then what I’m saying won’t make sense, so I circle around.\n\nI don’t know if other people think I’m not making sense. Nobody has said it to me that way, not directly. But I do get worried I’m not explaining it right, especially when I’m trying to talk about something that already feels confusing to me. Then I kind of over-explain, and halfway through I can hear myself doing it, but stopping feels hard because I’m afraid I’ll leave out the important part.\n\nSo… hard to express my thoughts, maybe sometimes. But I don’t know if my thoughts themselves are broken up, or if I’m just anxious about saying them clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your mood changes when you hear the voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it does. It’s not always in a simple way, like the voice happens and then I immediately feel one exact thing. But I’ve noticed sometimes when it’s around, or when I’m already kind of keyed up, my mood can shoot way up or flip into irritation really fast.\n\nLike, I’ll be at work and suddenly feel like I’m on top of everything, like I could fix every car in the whole shop by myself, like nobody can keep up with me and I know exactly what needs doing. And then if somebody touches my socket set or moves something from where I had it, I don’t just get mildly annoyed — I blow up. It feels personal, like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back.\n\nAnd at home it can be the same kind of thing. I’ll feel like I’m going a hundred miles an hour, like I’m handling everything, and then my husband asks something normal, like why the dishes aren’t done, and I snap at him like he’s attacking me. In the moment it feels justified, like he doesn’t understand what I’m dealing with, but afterward I can see it was way bigger than the situation called for.\n\nSo… yes, my mood changes. It can get really high, and then really irritable, especially around when the voice is happening or when I’m already wound up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a different person when the voice is talking to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’m a completely different person, but it feels like something shifts in me when it’s happening. Like I’m still me, I know I’m me, but when that man’s voice starts coming through — usually from the garage vents, or sometimes when I’m leaned over a hood and it feels like it’s coming from under there — it’s so clear that it kind of takes over the room, if that makes sense.\n\nIt doesn’t feel like a thought I’m having. It sounds like somebody is right next to me, talking straight at me, and then I start reacting like I would if an actual person were there saying those things. Especially when he tells me not to trust my husband, or tells me not to touch certain cars. I start feeling on edge, like I have to listen or at least pay attention, because ignoring it feels… risky, I guess.\n\nSo, different person? Maybe not exactly. But I do feel less like myself. More guarded. More suspicious. Like my normal judgment gets pushed to the side for a while because the voice is so convincing in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're doing things because the voice told you to, without being able to choose not to do them?\n\nPatient: It’s hard to explain, because I don’t feel like my body is being remote-controlled or anything like that. I still feel like I’m the one standing there, making the choice. But when the voice is really clear — like from the garage vents, or when I’m bent over a hood and it sounds like a man is right there next to me — it can feel like I’m supposed to listen.\n\nEspecially when he tells me not to touch certain cars. I’ll stop what I’m doing, or hesitate, or kind of back off for a minute because it feels wrong to ignore it. Same with when he says not to trust my husband — I don’t want to just believe it, but it gets in my head and I start acting more guarded.\n\nSo I don’t know if I’d say I can’t choose not to. But in the moment, it feels really hard to go against it, because it sounds so real. Like someone standing there warning me, not just a thought I can brush off.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I noticed it right away, because at first I’d tell myself I was just tired or busy or needed a quiet day, but then the quiet days kind of turn into stretches where I’m not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I used to be pretty involved with church stuff — the potlucks especially. I’d help plan them, bring food, stay after to clean up, talk to people. It wasn’t some huge glamorous thing, but it mattered to me. Lately I’ll see the calls or texts come in and I just… don’t answer. I tell myself I’ll call back later, and then I don’t. And then I feel embarrassed, so I avoid it more.\n\nEven at work, sometimes I’ll be in the bay and just kind of stare at my tools. Tools I know, tools I’ve used a thousand times, and I’ll just sit there looking at them like I can’t make myself care enough to pick them up. It’s not that I don’t know what to do. It’s more like the part of me that used to feel interested or motivated just goes flat.\n\nAt home too, I’ll sit in the house and not call anyone, not go anywhere, not want to do the things I used to say I liked. It’s like everything feels too far away from me, even stuff that used to feel normal. So yes… I think I have lost interest, at least in these stretches.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice is actually trying to protect you or warn you about something real?\n\nPatient: I go back and forth on that. When I’m sitting here talking to you, part of me knows it sounds… I don’t know, it sounds strange to say out loud. But when it’s happening, it really does feel like he’s warning me about something real.\n\nWhat brought me in is that I keep hearing this man’s voice at work, and it’s not like an inside thought or my own worries. It comes from the garage vents sometimes, like above or off to the side, and other times when I’m leaned over under the hood it sounds like it’s coming from right there, under the hood, clear as somebody standing next to me. And he’ll say things like not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. So in the moment, yeah, it feels like maybe he knows something I don’t.\n\nI don’t want to sound like I’m just blindly believing it, because I do question it afterward. But when it’s that clear, and it’s saying “don’t touch that” or warning me about my husband, it feels hard to treat it like nothing. It feels like if I ignore it and something happens, then that would be on me. So… yes, sometimes I do believe it’s trying to protect me, or at least warn me. Even if another part of me is scared that I don’t really know where it’s coming from.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice ever give you instructions on what actions to take?\n\nPatient: Yes… sometimes it does. Not like, “go do this exact complicated thing,” but it gives me instructions in the sense that it tells me what not to do.\n\nThe main thing is at work. I’ll hear this man’s voice coming from the garage vents, or sometimes when I’m under the hood it sounds like it’s right there with me, clear as somebody standing beside me. And he’ll say not to touch certain cars. Like I’ll be about to start working on one, or reach for something, and I hear it, and then I stop. I might step back or just freeze for a second because it feels like I’m being told there’s a reason I shouldn’t.\n\nAnd he’s told me not to trust my husband too. That’s not an action like picking up a tool, but it still affects what I do, because then I get guarded or start questioning things.\n\nSo yes, I guess the answer is yes — he does give instructions, mostly warnings or “don’t do that” kinds of things. It sounds so real when it happens that it’s hard not to react to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been showing less emotion than usual, even when something good or bad happens?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s hard to explain because it’s not like I’m choosing to be cold or I don’t understand that something matters. It’s more like my face and my voice don’t always catch up to what’s happening, or maybe I’m not feeling it the way I normally would.\n\nThere are these stretches where I just get really flat. Like people will call or text me, and I see it, and I know I should answer, especially people from church or friends who are probably just checking in. And I just… don’t. I let it sit there. Then I feel bad, but even the feeling bad is kind of dull, like it’s under a blanket.\n\nThe church potlucks are a good example. I used to help with those a lot — planning, bringing food, talking to people, staying after. It used to make me feel connected, like I was part of something. Now I’ll skip them, even when I know I should go, and I don’t have some big reaction about missing it. I just sit at home. Sometimes I’m sitting there knowing I’m missing something I used to care about, and I still can’t make myself feel much.\n\nAt work it happens too. I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and these are tools I know, work I know how to do, and I’ll just look at them like they’re somebody else’s. Like I can’t make myself care enough to start. I don’t get excited, I don’t get frustrated in the normal way… I just kind of go blank.\n\nSo yes, less emotion, I guess. Or less showing it. Maybe both. Everything gets muted.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you show less emotion than usual when the voice is not present?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think it still happens even when I’m not hearing him. It’s not only when the voice is there and I’m scared or distracted. There are whole stretches where it’s just me, no voice right then, and I still feel… flat, I guess. Like I’m moving through the day but not really attached to anything.\n\nLike with my phone — I’ll see people have called or texted, and it’s not because the voice is telling me not to answer in that moment. I just look at it and can’t make myself care enough to pick it up, even though I know I should. Or the church potlucks, I used to help run those, like actually be involved and talk to people and bring things, and now I’ll just skip them and sit in the house. Not crying, not angry, just sitting there like everything is far away.\n\nAnd at work too, even when it’s quiet and nothing is happening, I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and I know what I’m supposed to do, I know those tools, but I feel like I can’t make myself care about any of it. It’s there on its own sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself feeling more emotional or connected to things when you're not in one of those flat stretches?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. I mean… I don’t want to make it sound like I’m flat every single second, because I’m not. There are times where I do feel more like myself, or I’ll notice something matters to me again for a little while.\n\nBut when those stretches hit, it’s like everything just shuts down. I won’t call anyone back, even if I know they care about me. I’ll see the phone light up and just let it go. And the church potlucks… I used to help with those, not just show up, but actually help run things, talk to people, bring food, clean up after. When I’m not in that flat place, I can remember why that mattered. I can even miss it a little.\n\nBut during it, I just skip it. I sit in the house and don’t really do anything with the feeling. Or at work, I’ll be in the bay staring at my tools, and I know exactly what I’m supposed to be doing, but it’s like I can’t make myself care enough to start. It feels disconnected, like there’s a wall between me and everything I normally care about.\n\nSo yes, I guess there are times I feel more connected than that. But the flat stretches are strong enough that they kind of take over when they’re happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt extremely energetic, irritable, or abnormally happy for several days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s not just like a normal good mood, either. It’s more like I get this rush where I feel like I’m on top of everything, like I could handle anything anybody throws at me.\n\nAt work, I’ll be flying high, like I can fix every car in town if they just bring them to my bay. I’ll feel fast and sharp and almost… untouchable, I guess. Like nobody else is keeping up with me. And in those times, if something small happens, it can flip really quick. Like if another tech moves my socket set or puts something back wrong, I don’t just get annoyed — I blow up like they’re doing it on purpose, like they’re trying to slow me down or hold me back.\n\nAnd at home it’s the same kind of thing. I can be feeling really up, really energized, like I’ve got all these things I’m going to do, and then if my husband asks something simple like why the dishes aren’t done, I take it like he’s criticizing me or trying to box me in. And I know I kind of said this already, but it’s hard to explain without making it sound like I’m just “in a good mood,” because it’s not really that. It’s more like I’m flying high, like I’m suddenly faster than everybody else and I can handle anything.\n\nLike at work, I’ll feel like I can fix every car in town if they’d just bring them to my bay.
